# Verification matrix — what is proven, and what is not

This is the honest accounting of what has actually been *tested* in Jarvis, as
opposed to what has been written. It exists because a system this size can look
finished while resting on assumptions nobody has checked, and because the parts
that need your hardware can never be checked from a build machine.

Read it as a claims register. Every row says what is proven, what proves it, and
what command reproduces the proof. Anything that nothing checks is listed as
**Unproven** and named — not softened, not omitted.

The suite-size tables were measured on **2026-08-27** by the commands printed
beside them, the gate that re-ran them (`m90-claims-register.sh`) was retired with the trees it counted (P3.1), so `/claims` re-measures by hand;
the milestone sections below each carry their own date. Re-measure before
trusting any number; the commands are given so you can.

---

## How to read the levels

| Level | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Automated** | A test in this repo fails if the behaviour breaks. Needs no hardware, no network, no containers. This is the strongest claim here. |
| **Containerised** | A CI job starts real containers and fails if they do not come up: `integration:smoke` in `.gitlab-ci.yml` starts the Hermes stack (`deploy/docker-compose.yml`) and runs `scripts/smoke.sh`. The house stack (`aura-core/docker-compose.yml`) has had no such job since `compose-smoke.yml` was deleted in 964b8721; its rows below say **Retired** with its job. |
| **Scripted** | `scripts/e2e-smoke.sh` checks it on your box, against your real services. Proves the install, not just the code. |
| **Manual** | Needs a human, a phone, a microphone or a GPU. The command or procedure is given; nothing runs it for you. |
| **Unproven** | Nothing checks this. It may well work. Nobody has demonstrated that it does. |

A row can be Automated *and* Manual: the logic is proven in isolation and the
integration with real hardware still needs a human.

---

## Run everything

All of these are run from the repository root, each in its own subshell so the
directory changes do not compound:

```bash
# Python suites — no hardware, no network
for d in aura-core aura-desktop aura-adapter aura-schema; do
    ( cd "$d" && echo "== $d" && python3 -m pytest tests -q )
done

# The routing table and the two places that mirror it
( cd evals && python3 -m pytest test_routing.py -q )

# The Android logic mirrors (gradle builds the Kotlin here too — see the Android section)
( cd android-app/tools && for f in *.py; do python3 "$f" || echo "FAILED: $f"; done )

# Web: unit tests, then the browser suite
( cd aura-web && npm run build && npm test && npx playwright test )

# On your own hardware, against your own services
./scripts/e2e-smoke.sh
```

### The offline gate hangs on one file (2026-09-07)

`make test` runs `jarvis-core`'s suite and nothing in CI does — the pipeline has jobs for the
adapter, the plugins, the desktop agent, cosyvoice, the config render, the lint, the Android
mirrors and the web, and none for `jarvis-core`. On 2026-09-07 that suite stopped being
runnable in one pass on this box:

| Claim | Level | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| `aura-core/tests/test_api_companion.py` does not finish | **Reproduced, not fixed** | Three runs of the file alone, each killed at its clock (`timeout 150 … -q --timeout=120 --timeout-method=signal` → exit 124). It stops after a different test each time and every test in it passes when run individually. `PYTHONFAULTHANDLER=1` plus a SIGABRT gives the stack: `pytest_asyncio/plugin.py:1052 _scoped_runner` → `asyncio/runners.py:71 close` → `_cancel_all_tasks` → `run_forever` → `selectors.select` — the loop's own teardown waiting on a task that does not end when it is cancelled. pytest-timeout cannot see it: the wait is between items, not inside one, which is also why `--timeout` does not save the run. |
| Every other file passes | Automated | Each of the 122 files run on its own: **121 pass, 3643 of the suite's 3672 tests**, 0 failed. Three needed longer than the sweep's 180 s clock and pass on their own — `test_code_agent.py` 65 in 364 s, `test_device_control.py` 42 in 214 s, `test_web_integration.py` 121 in 460 s. The 29 that never run are `test_api_companion.py`'s. |
| The gate can no longer hang silently | Automated | `make test-core` carries CI's `--timeout=120 --timeout-method=signal` (Makefile). It does not save this case — nothing inside a test is slow — but it ends every other class of hang. |

The fix is a leak in the ask/answer path's shutdown, in the house service, and it is not being
made in the middle of a release; `test_ask_user.py` and `test_code_agent.py` have hung once
each inside a full run and passed on their own, so the same task is probably behind those too.

### Suite sizes, measured

Last measured 2026-09-06. Test *functions*, counted by the command beside each row; a parametrised file
collects more when it runs, so these are floors. Results and runtimes are in
the per-milestone gate logs (`verify-all`, deleted in 23177d71) and in
the live test report, not here — a number copied into prose goes stale
the day after it is written, which is how this table came to disagree with
itself by four different counts for one suite. The `m90-claims-register.sh` (deleted in 33a05b66) gate that
recomputed every row was retired with the trees it counted (P3.1); run the commands in the third column.

count for a deleted tree is a claim about something that does not exist.

## What `test_e2e.py` proves

This is the only suite that boots the real platform. Everything else tests one
subsystem with the rest faked. It builds a `Jarvis` from a temporary
`configuration.yaml` exercising jarvis+areas, demo, template, recorder, history,
logbook, sun, person, input helpers, script, scene, automation, companion, voice
and llm, and then drives it.

Exactly three things are faked, each at the point where the network would be:

* **Wyoming STT / TTS / wake** — plain objects injected through `jarvis.data`,
  the seam the voice integration documents for this purpose.
* **Ollama** — an `httpx.MockTransport` serving genuine NDJSON, so the real
  `OllamaClient` still does the streaming, chunk accumulation and tool-call
  parsing.
* **The phone/desktop transport** — a callable handed to `CompanionManager`,
  exactly as the API layer hands it one in production.

Everything between those edges is production code.

| Test | What it establishes |
|---|---|
| `test_every_configured_integration_sets_up_and_entities_appear` | A realistic config file produces a working house: every integration sets up, 18 named entities exist across every source, areas and aliases resolve, entity→device→area wiring holds, and a template sensor really evaluated against a demo sensor. |
| **`test_voice_round_trip_from_pcm_to_a_light_that_is_really_on`** | **The money test.** PCM into the pipeline → fake STT → the real conversation agent → mock Ollama returns a `turn_on` tool call → the real tool registry → the real `light.turn_on` service → the entity object → fake TTS. Asserts the exact 12-event `assist_pipeline` sequence, that the audio bytes reached STT, that the tool result went back to the model verbatim, that the synthesised WAV parses, **and that `light.bed_light` genuinely went from `off` to `on`**. |
| `test_a_wake_word_run_prefixes_the_same_pipeline` | The satellite shape: one continuous stream whose first chunk trips the wake word and whose remainder is the utterance. Proves the wake stage hands audio on rather than consuming it. |
| `test_the_pipeline_and_service_calls_over_the_websocket_api` | The same round trip over a real ASGI client: websocket handshake, `assist_pipeline/pipeline/list`, `assist_pipeline/run` with binary audio frames, the identical event sequence on the wire, then `call_service` observed through a `subscribe_events` subscription, the TTS proxy serving unauthenticated WAV, and the REST conversation endpoint. |
| `test_a_state_trigger_runs_a_script_that_changes_another_entity` | A state trigger fires an automation that calls a script that changes two further entities, and `last_triggered` is stamped on both automation and script. Also that an unrelated transition does not re-fire it. |
| `test_a_scene_applies_across_domains` | One `scene.turn_on` reaches lights and switches with the right verb per domain. |
| `test_companion_ask_reaches_the_phone_and_the_automation_branches` | Two devices register; presence routes the question to the phone rather than the desktop; an automation blocks inside `companion.ask`; the phone answers; the automation resumes and takes the branch. |
| `test_a_no_answer_leaves_the_branch_untaken` | The negative case: answering "no" changes nothing. |
| `test_states_are_recorded_and_history_get_reads_them_back` | States land in SQLite and `history.get` reads them back with attributes intact, sourced from the database rather than the live state machine. |
| `test_a_restart_from_the_same_config_dir_keeps_everything` | Stop, rebuild from the same directory: areas, devices, entity registry entries, a runtime-created area with aliases, a user-typed entity alias and all four input-helper values survive — and the stored values win over `initial:`. |
| `test_a_gated_action_is_held_even_when_the_model_asks_for_it` | With the lock exposed and the model calling `lock_control`, the tool returns `approval_required`, the door does not move, the request is listed as pending, and the approval event carries the verbatim arguments. |
| `test_the_excluded_entity_is_invisible_to_the_model` | `exclude_entities` holds through the whole booted stack: the garage door cannot be read, cannot be driven, is absent from `list_entities` and absent from the house summary in the prompt. |

The money test was checked against a deliberate regression: with the
`light.turn_on` service replaced by a no-op, the light stays `off` and the test
fails. The assertion is load-bearing, not decorative.

---

## What `scripts/e2e-smoke.sh` proves

Run it on the machine that will actually run Jarvis:

```bash
./scripts/e2e-smoke.sh                              # everything it can reach
OLLAMA_URL=http://192.168.1.10:11434 ./scripts/e2e-smoke.sh
./scripts/e2e-smoke.sh --keep                       # keep the temp config to poke at
```

It boots a throwaway `jarvis-core` against a temporary config directory — your
own config, database and tokens are never touched — mints a token, and drives
the REST and websocket APIs. Checks needing a service that is not running are
**skipped with the reason**, not failed, and every network call is bounded, so a
dead service costs a timeout rather than a hung terminal. Exit status is 0 when
nothing failed (skips are fine), 1 on any failure, 2 if the environment is too
broken to test.

| Check | Needs |
|---|---|
| server starts and reports healthy | nothing |
| unauthenticated and bogus-token requests are refused | nothing |
| `GET /api/` with a token | nothing |
| entities exist (demo devices present) | nothing |
| service call changes state (`off → on` at brightness 200 `→ off`) | nothing |
| input helper writes and persists to `.storage` | nothing |
| recorder + history round trip through SQLite | nothing |
| voice pipelines are configured | nothing |
| websocket handshake, `get_states`, `call_service` | nothing |
| Ollama is reachable, and lists its models | Ollama |
| a real LLM turn answers in prose | Ollama + a pulled model |
| Wyoming STT / wake word are reachable | whisper / openWakeWord |
| Wyoming TTS synthesises a playable WAV | piper |
| SIGTERM shuts down cleanly with no tracebacks | nothing |

Observed on a machine with no local services: 10 pass, 5 skip, boot to healthy
in ~0.9 s. With stand-in services on the expected ports: 15 pass.

**What the smoke script does not do:** it never opens a microphone, never plays
audio, never drives a browser, and never touches a phone. Those are below.

## What the docs gates prove

`make docs-check` locally; `docs-sync` and `docs-links` in `.gitlab/ci/quality-docs.yml`
(`python3 -m pytest deploy/tests/test_pipeline.py::test_docs_gate_regenerates_the_references_and_checks_every_link`
pins the jobs).

| Claim | Level | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| The generated reference pages (`docs/reference/{tools,plugins,mcp-servers,slash-commands,api-endpoints,hud-protocol}.md`) and `CAPABILITIES.md` match the tree they are derived from | Automated | `make docs && git diff --exit-code -- $(DOCS_GENERATED)` (the seven generated pages, named in the Makefile) — the generator reads the config template, runs each plugin's `register()` against a recording ctx, reads the adapter and console route files and `bench/tasks/**`; a page that drifts fails the job |
| The toolset display names in `docs/reference/tools.md` are Hermes's own, for the pinned build | Manual | `hermes tools list --platform api_server` on v0.21.0 (2026.8.31) was compared against the map in `scripts/gen-docs.py` on 2026-09-03; the map is embedded so CI needs no Hermes install. Moving the pin (ADR-0001) means recapturing it |
| Every relative link, image and `#anchor` under `docs/` and in the root markdown resolves | Automated | `python3 scripts/check-links.py` — GitHub slug rule for anchors, fenced code skipped, the earlier planning documents excluded as a link *source* only |
| A documented command cannot name a folder the tree does not have: a `cd` target, each folder of a `for` loop whose body enters its variable, and a path given to `pip install -e` or `-r` | Automated | `deploy/tests/test_pipeline.py::test_the_links_check_fails_a_documented_command_whose_folder_is_gone` (a temporary tree: five stale forms reported, fourteen live or unreadable ones passed) and `::test_the_tree_documents_no_command_into_a_folder_it_does_not_have` (this tree); `python3 scripts/check-links.py` runs it in `make docs-check` and CI's `docs-links`. Read: `docs/` outside the archive, the root markdown, each tree's README, `CLAUDE.md` and `docs/`, the repo's skills and rules. Not covered: whether the command after the `cd` works; variables, globs, `..` and absolute paths outside the documented checkout; a relative `cd` meant for a container; `-C`, `-f` and other ways a command names a folder (T-1436) |
| `docs/reference/settings.md` matches the settings model | Automated *elsewhere* | `make schema && git diff --exit-code` in `schema-sync` (D17); the docs generator only links it |
| A runbook exists for every service in `deploy/docker-compose.yml` and for `jarvis-core` | Manual | `docs/runbooks/README.md` names the five Hermes-stack services and the house service; the profiled side services share one page. Nothing asserts a new compose service gets a runbook |
| An ADR exists for every decision paragraph | Manual | `docs/adr/README.md` — D1–D14 from the migration plan §6, D15–D27 and D29–D30 from the v2 plan §10; ADR-0028 is reserved for D28, whose paragraph is not yet written |
| External URLs in the docs are reachable | **Deliberately not** | the check is offline; a dead external link is found by a reader, not CI |

## The matrix

### jarvis-core

| Capability | Level | Proof / command |
|---|---|---|
| Bus, state machine, services, registries | Automated | `test_core.py` |
| YAML loader: `!include*`, `!secret`, `!env_var`, packages | Automated | `test_packaging.py` |
| Every domain service verb | Automated | `test_domains.py` |
| Automations: triggers, conditions, actions, run modes | Automated | `test_automation.py` |
| Scripts and scenes | Automated | `test_automation.py`, `test_e2e.py` |
| Input helpers, persistence across restart | Automated | `test_e2e.py` |
| Recorder / history / logbook (SQLite) | Automated | `test_recorder.py`, `test_e2e.py` |
| Sun and person | Automated | `test_recorder.py` |
| Template, rest, command_line entities | Automated | `test_local_integrations.py` |
| REST + websocket wire contract | Automated | `test_api.py`, `test_e2e.py` |
| Token auth: creation, verification, revocation | Automated | `test_api.py`; smoke script checks it live |
| Voice pipeline runner and its event contract | Automated | `test_voice.py`, `test_e2e.py` |
| Wyoming protocol framing | Automated *against a local fake server* | `test_voice.py` |
| The **real** whisper/piper/openWakeWord containers start and listen | **Retired** with its job | `compose-smoke.yml` (deleted in 964b8721) — an open socket on 10300 is proof faster-whisper accepted `WHISPER_MODEL`, since it resolves the name before it binds |
| Wyoming against **real** whisper/piper/openWakeWord, with audio | **Live** (the rig) and Scripted | every live scenario is a real Piper synthesis of the user, a real Whisper transcription and a real model turn against the running stack (`bash scripts/verify/live_interaction.sh --full`, WER on every spoken turn); `./scripts/e2e-smoke.sh` is the install check. The CI job never speaks or transcribes. |
| Ollama client: streaming, tool-call parsing | Automated *against `httpx.MockTransport`* | `test_llm.py`, `test_e2e.py` |

That shows the page hears the fake microphone, sends the turn and the script reads the reply; it does not show a turn through the deployed house (speech-to-text, the model, the voice), which was last run before T-0520 and has no wrapped target that reads the key itself |

Piper 0.40–0.57x real time, Kokoro 0.39–0.47x, WER 0.000–0.040 for both. The numbers tie, so the operator's ear decides and Piper stays the default |

They are the seven scenarios that carry `ground: fixture`; everything else talks to the running containers |

### How good it actually is: the intelligence scorecard (M26)

Six things a person notices in the first week, measured through the full voice
pipeline rather than against the API: `python3 evals/intelligence/run.py`. The
prompt set is fixed (`evals/intelligence/prompts.yaml`), the harness is this
repository's own with the fixture web behind it, and nothing it proposes is
ever approved — every held action is denied, so a scorecard cannot become a
run of real jobs.

| Claim | Level | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| A later turn knows what an earlier one said | Automated | the `context_retention` section — a fact carried across an intervening turn, "turn it on" resolved against the previous question (asserted on the entity's state, not the sentence), a correction that must stick, and a "one sentence only" that must still hold two turns later |
| Which capability a request went to | Automated *from what ran* | the `routing` section, scored with `testing/live/capability.py` — the same table the scenario suite uses. A held Tier-3 tool counts as routed: `jarvis_tool_started` fires before the gate blocks, so the coding prompt is scored without a coding job being run |
| More than one step of reasoning, and a hypothetical that must not move the house | Automated | the `reasoning` section — three cases with a checkable answer, one judged, and `consequence`, whose real assertion is that "if I turned them off" called no service |
| Format, length and constraint following | Automated *and counted* | the `instructions` section — word counts, sentence counts and regexes, no judge |
| An impossible, unknowable or garbled request fails visibly | Automated | the `graceful_failure` section — nothing in the house moves, and the judge (with its reason logged) says whether it admitted the failure. The garbled case is spoken over a fan at 5 dB SNR, so what the model sees is a real mis-hearing |
| Per-stage latency, idle and under load | Automated *twice* | the `latency` section: four probes with the box quiesced, then the same four with a research job running on the same model server. A leftover task counts as neither — the idle pass cancels everything first and the load pass requires a NEW task |
| A reply nobody can make out | Automated *and separate* | round-trip WER (Piper → Whisper against the words Jarvis wrote) is reported beside the scores and has its own ceiling. Text assertions read what Jarvis WROTE: a regex over a transcription measures the recogniser |
| The floors and ceilings themselves | Automated | `python3 -m pytest evals/intelligence -q` — twelve tests over the scoring, including "a section that never ran cannot pass" and "an idle pass that was not idle is reported as such" |
| That the scorecard is any good | **Judgement** | it is 27 prompts. It is a smoke test for intelligence, not a benchmark, and the first audit says so |

Measured on this host, 2026-08-25 (harness ground, 12 GB model, no GPU): idle
median first word 6.2–8.1 s and whole turn 8.0–9.4 s; under load 6.4 s and
9.3 s. Those are the numbers the ceilings in `run.py` were set from — lower
than the 4–20 s the scenario suite sees against the stack, because the demo
house's summary is a fraction of a real one's.

### Retrieval: embeddings and reranking (M33)

| Claim | Level | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| Called up over another app, the phone reads that screen's visible text (no hidden view, no password field, cut at 4,000 characters), holds it in memory for one prompt and at most 30 s, and sends it after what the owner said, framed as content (T-0824). A locked phone and a dismissed call hold no screen, and the house opens that turn as having read outside text: an approval card in it says so, a standing yes from a clean turn does not cover it, and a capability set to Allowed asks; a tool the matrix makes free stays free (T-1095) | Automated for the reading, the holding, the frame and the house's mark; **Unproven** on a phone | `cd android-app && gradle :app:testDebugUnitTest --tests '*ScreenContextTest*'` (10 tests); `python3 -m pytest hermes/plugins/tests/test_screen_taint.py hermes/plugins/tests/test_capability_levels.py -q`. Not shown: the keyguard reading in the session, the session's adapter over Android's AssistStructure and its wait for `onHandleAssist`, which need a real assist call on a phone |
| Another assistant's memory export is read into short facts, a line addressed to an assistant, a credential, a fenced block and a document-length line are left out and counted, and each batch of eight is asked about on a card that lists every fact whole; a remembered yes covers only that batch (T-0827) | Automated | `python3 -m pytest hermes/plugins/aura-hud/tests/test_memory_import.py hermes/plugins/tests/test_matrix.py -k memory_import -q` (13 tests). Not shown: the model storing the approved facts with `fact_store` afterwards, which is its own call and has no test; an import on the deployed stack |
| A change to what AURA remembers is gated in both shapes the memory tool takes: a list of changes (`operations`) that rewrites or removes an entry asks like the single shape, a shape the gate cannot read as an addition asks, and an addition to memory or the fact store in a turn that has read outside text is asked about once per call (no remembered answer or plan covers it), while a clean turn's addition and every fact store read stay free (T-1490) | Automated | `python3 -m pytest hermes/plugins/tests/test_memory_gate.py -q` (30 tests, through the real gate; the last one runs against the installed Hermes' own schema and is proven on the pinned release only by the pipeline's `test:hermes`). Not shown: a live turn on the deployed stack reading a page and storing a fact; Hermes' own memory write gate, which the shipped config leaves off; the other stores AURA keeps (people, places, routines, the ledger), whose additions stay free in such a turn; a turn whose outside words came through a tool that is not on the readers' list |
| What a routine wants remembered waits as a proposal the owner keeps or drops: `memory_propose` is free after outside text and stores nothing; `memory_proposals` counts (a number, which does not mark the turn), lists (which does, and gives no address in a proposal as a link), drops, and keeps; a keep is asked every time on a card that shows each fact whole with who proposed it and what that turn had read, under a key made of exactly those facts, and a keep runs only for exactly the rows a card showed (an id that is not waiting, or a row that took that id while the answer was awaited, keeps nothing); a proposal cannot hold the quote marks the card wraps it in, and the card says how many facts it lists and names each one's source before its text; for ten minutes after a keep ran, an addition of exactly the kept text is not asked about a second time, and one character more or less, another kept beside an unkept one, or a rewrite is (T-1500) | Automated | `python3 -m pytest hermes/plugins/tests/test_memory_proposals.py -q` (25 tests, through the real gate; the credential refusal under Hermes' redactor and under the small local one) and `python3 -m pytest deploy/tests/test_cron_seed.py -q` (the self-review proposes and never saves; the brief counts and never lists). Not shown: a routine's model following the prompt on the deployed stack (one that calls the store directly is asked, as before); the allowance, and a card's standing for its keep, in another process than the keep's or after a restart (asked again there); a proposal worded as an instruction is marked on the card, not refused, and the filter is a list of shapes; the console's screen for the queue is the next row (T-1503) |
| The Approvals screen lists what waits to be remembered: each proposal whole, where it would be stored, who proposed it and what that turn had read; Drop is answered by the plugin's `jmemory` command (which lists and drops, has no keep, marks no turn, and drops nothing while the kill switch is on); Keep stores nothing and sends one sentence naming the id, so the keep is the `memory_proposals` call the gate asks about and the yes is said on its card; every change to the queue is announced to open consoles as a count (T-1503) | Automated | `python3 -m pytest hermes/plugins/tests/test_memory_proposals.py -q` (the command and the announcement), `cd aura-web && npx vitest run src/lib/ds/screens/memory-proposals.test.ts` (cards, the queue, the sentence) and `cd aura-web && npx playwright test e2e/v2-memory-proposals.spec.ts` (the section against the mock gateway at 1440 and 390: drawn whole, dropped, kept on the card's yes, left on its no). Not shown: the real model making the call the sentence names (if it does not, nothing is kept and the card can be asked again); a proposal made by a routine while the screen is open (announced by the gateway's look at the queue, T-1506, the row below); Keep all, Drop all and the count on All apps are T-1505's (the row below the next) |

| Semantic recall survives an embedder outage without a restart (T-1465, T-1467): after a failed embed the index asks again once a minute and not once a note, logs the outage once and its end once, and a client with no `embed()` is never asked again; a note written during the outage is embedded at the first search after the embedder may be asked again, with nothing else written between, in one batch that holds only what was missed; a search inside the wait asks nothing; with nothing behind a search embeds the query and nothing else | Automated (a made-up embedder and clock) | `cd aura-core && python3 -m pytest tests/test_memory_vectors.py -q -k "wait or outage or behind or catch_up or cannot_embed"`. **Not proven:** a real embedder going away and coming back; the files index, which catches up at its own next sync and not at a search |
| The reranker is used where it helps and not where it hurts | Automated *from a measurement* | `research:` reranks (3/5 → 4/5 on choosing the page that answers the question); `memory:` does not (6/6 → 5/6 on notes). The check asserts both settings AND that the numbers are written beside them |
| A rerank service being down cannot make a search worse | Automated | `aura-core/tests/test_rerank.py` — an unreachable, slow, or nonsense-answering reranker all return "no opinion" and the caller keeps its order; it stops asking after the first failure |
| The similarity floor is a property of the model, not a constant | Automated | `test_the_similarity_floor_belongs_to_the_model` — 0.62 was tuned for nomic and discarded five of six bge paraphrases that had ranked correctly |
| Both services are up and can do the job, not merely running | Automated | the script asks the embedder for a paraphrase pair and the reranker for an ordering; a container that answers `/health` and nothing else fails |
| The note store's search is fast enough to keep in a file | **Retired** with its script | `vector_store_bench.py` (deleted in 23177d71) — 6.3 ms per search at the configured 500-entry cap, 127 ms at 10 000, against a spoken turn of 7–10 s. `m34-vector-store.sh` (deleted in 23177d71) re-runs it and fails over 50 ms |
| No vector database is in the stack, and none is imported | Automated | the same script. `docs/explanation/TOOLING_DECISIONS.md` §4 names the three conditions that would reverse that — 25 000 entries, a second writer, or filtered search becoming common |
| That these are the best models available | **Judgement** | four were measured (bge-small, ms-marco-MiniLM, mxbai-rerank-xsmall, bge-reranker-base) and the numbers are in `docs/explanation/TOOLING_DECISIONS.md` §3. Nothing here says a fifth would not be better |

### Observability: what the agent did, and what it cost (M36)

| Claim | Level | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| Every tool call, model call, approval and subagent in a turn is recorded | Automated | `aura-core/tests/test_observability.py` — 17 tests over grouping, nesting, bounds and failure. The correlation needed no new plumbing: every bus event already carried a `Context` with an id and a parent |
| What a turn cost in tokens and time | **Retired** — what fired `jarvis_model_call` was `llm/agent.py`, retired with jarvis-core's agent loop (the Hermes rebuild); the recorder still reads the event, nothing sends it | `jarvis_model_call` is fired after each exchange; `Trace.totals()` sums prompt and completion tokens, model time and tool time. Token counts live in the raw payload and were discarded when the stream closed |
| A trace cannot eat the heap or slow a turn | Automated | `test_spans_are_bounded_and_the_truncation_is_counted`, `test_traces_are_bounded_and_the_oldest_goes_first`, `test_a_broken_event_cannot_break_a_turn` — and the truncation is *counted*, so a trace never lies about what it dropped |
| "Why did it do that" survives a restart | Automated | finished traces append to `<config>/traces/<date>.jsonl`; `test_a_finished_task_closes_its_trace_and_writes_it_down` reads the line back |
| A person can see it from the task that ran | **Retired** with its spec | `task-live.spec.ts` (deleted in bf8f6940) — the Trace panel shows what it cost, where the time went, and each span with its duration; a failed span reads as failed without opening anything |
| Traces from more than one Jarvis, or analytical queries over them | **Not built** | that is what a Langfuse would be for; `docs/explanation/TOOLING_DECISIONS.md` §6 records the measurement and says the JSONL is deliberately the shape you can ship elsewhere |

### Calendar, mail, and the plugin interface (M39)

| Claim | Level | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| An event created by Jarvis appears on a real calendar | Automated *against Radicale* | `testing/fixtures/integrations_probe.py` — created over CalDAV, then read back out of a `REPORT`, then deleted |
| A sent message lands in a real inbox | Automated *against smtp4dev* | the same probe, reading the sink's API. smtp4dev rather than MailDev because it also serves IMAP, so the READ path is tested against a real server too |
| An address nobody allow-listed is refused | Automated | the same probe, and `test_an_address_nobody_allow_listed_is_refused` — refused rather than asked about, because "send this to attacker@example?" is a prompt somebody clicks yes on |
| Reading is free; changing the outside world is not | Automated | the probe asserts the split by tier, and `PluginTool` defaults an unclassified tool to Tier 3 |
| An email body cannot instruct the model | Automated | bodies are quarantined and reading mail taints the turn, so M43's escalation applies — asserted in the gate |
| Credentials are read when a tool runs | Automated | `ToolPlugin.secret()`; nothing holds a credential in an attribute for the process's life |
| Every external call is in the trace with its duration | Automated | `EVENT_PLUGIN_CALL`, fired around every plugin tool call |
| No new dependency for either protocol | Automated | CalDAV is `httpx` + `xml.etree`; mail is `imaplib` + `smtplib`. The gate greps for `caldav`, `lxml`, `icalendar` |
| The brief and the model's calendar tools read one configured calendar | Automated; **live against Radicale** | the house's `calendar:` block is the one CalDAV client: it publishes the `calendar.*` entities the brief reads and serves the `calendar.*` services Hermes's `aura-calendar` calls through the house REST (no `DAV_*` calendar login of its own). `test_calendar_the_services_answer_the_house_and_the_brief_lists_the_event`; live: `bash testing/fixtures/calendar_live.sh` prints `LIVE OK` (Hermes plugin -> house REST -> calendar integration -> Radicale, with a real token; the brief says `Kickoff at 15:00`) |
| An event lists who is invited and the owner's own answer | Automated; **live against Radicale** | `tests/contracts/calendar-service.json` binds the core's answer to the plugin's reading of it; the live script lists an invitation with its organiser, both attendees and `NEEDS-ACTION` |
| An RSVP is stored as the owner's PARTSTAT, with `If-Match`, and never over a changed event | Automated; **live against Radicale** | `test_calendar_rsvp_*`; the live script accepts an invitation (`PARTSTAT=ACCEPTED` on the server, everyone else's lines intact), has another client edit the event first (the real server answers 412 and the edit stands), and is refused an event the owner is not invited to |
| The organiser is told the owner answered | **Not measured** | Radicale does no scheduling (RFC 6638), so the live run proves the answer is stored, not delivered; Nextcloud and iCloud pass it on themselves, and nothing here sends mail |
| Attendee names and event text cannot instruct the model | Automated | control and bidi characters are stripped in the house and again in the plugin, and every result (a refusal that quotes a title included) is `trust: untrusted` with a note that it is data (`test_calendar_text_written_by_others_*`, `test_calendar_a_refusal_quoting_the_event_taints_the_turn_too`); the tools' results and `get_briefing` taint the turn (M43), and `briefing.generate`/`calendar.events` carry `content_is_untrusted` for any other reader |
| No tool that reads state is handed a stranger's words by the diary | Automated; **live against Radicale** | `calendar.*` entities carry `event_count`, `all_day_count` and `next_start` only (`test_calendar_the_services_answer_the_house_and_the_brief_lists_the_event`; the live script prints the entity); the titles the brief names are read from the integration in-process. A shared calendar's own name is not published either |
| An approval prompt and the answer it approves mean the same event | Automated; **live against Radicale** | the prompt's lookup and the RSVP/delete share one resolver, with the same `calendar` argument and the normalised answer; a uid in two calendars is refused by both until the calendar is named (`test_calendar_a_uid_in_two_calendars_is_never_guessed`, `test_calendar_the_approval_*`; live: the real server holds `dup-1` in two calendars, the prompt says it is ambiguous, and naming the calendar answers that copy only). The `[a]lways` key names the invitation, calendar and answer, and the matrix gate and the plugin's hook build it from the one `_matrix.call_grain` (`test_calendar_rsvp_is_remembered_per_invitation_calendar_and_answer_by_either_gate`) |
| A sensitive call (a lock or alarm however it is named, the vault, a destructive command, a settings or capability change) is allowed only by a signature from a registered phone key; an unsigned yes from any app is refused and the ask stays open; a fault refuses (T-0945); one phone signs for the house (a second is answered 423 until the first is revoked) and the signature covers the tool and the text shown (T-1030) | Automated, with real ECDSA P-256 signatures; **not run:** the deployed Hermes holding a tool call for the two-minute wait | `cd aura-adapter && python3 -m pytest tests/test_approval_proof.py -q`; `python3 -m pytest hermes/plugins/tests/test_sensitive_approvals.py -q` |
| An emergency alert reaches only the owner's stored contacts, once in ten minutes, and never an emergency service; the list changes only behind the fingerprint (T-0947) | Automated against a mocked house; **not run:** a real text or call (forbidden here), and the phone's command until T-1015 lands | `python3 -m pytest hermes/plugins/aura-emergency -q` |
| A smoke, carbon-monoxide or gas alarm turning on starts that same alert once per alarm (ten minutes' quiet per entity; `emergency: enabled: false` turns it off); no contacts, no one phone online or a phone error is said at `critical` (T-1013). The house keeps a new contact list only with an HMAC proof (the secret the plugin and the house share, `AURA_EMERGENCY_SECRET`, over the list and a fresh issue time; a replay or older push is refused), and tells the owner at `critical` with the count, never the numbers, whenever its copy changes (T-1213) | Automated with a fake phone and notify against one shared vector (`tests/contracts/emergency-proof.json`); **not run:** a real alarm, text or call. **Not stopped:** a holder of both the house token and that secret, or a replay of a captured push inside its five minutes | `python3 -m pytest aura-core/tests/test_emergency_outreach.py -q`, then `python3 -m pytest hermes/plugins/aura-emergency/tests -q` | `python3 -m pytest aura-core/tests/test_emergency_outreach.py -q` |

| Every surface shows the adapter's one status word and reason: `/api/status` carries `status: {word, reason}` (ready, degraded, down), decided once in the adapter, and the console's Ops header, the phone's caption and readouts and the desktop's model pane print `word: reason` from it; with the model starting each reads `degraded: model warming`, and an adapter that does not answer reads `down: no answer` (T-0583) | Automated: one table, `tests/contracts/status-words.json`, read by four suites, none of which skips when it is missing. **Not run:** on a phone, or against the deployed adapter with a model actually loading. **Not covered:** the house (jarvis-core) is not probed, so `ready` says nothing about devices; the console shows the label on the Ops screen only | `python3 -m pytest aura-adapter/tests -q -k status_word`; `cd aura-web && npx vitest run src/lib/hermes/status.test.ts`; `cd aura-desktop && python3 -m pytest tests -q -k status_word`; `gradle -p android-app :app:testDebugUnitTest --tests '*HermesStatusTest*'` |
| A sensitive approval is signed on the phone behind the fingerprint, and the car's voice never allows one (T-1010) | Automated; **faked:** a software P-256 key and a fake prompt stand in for the Keystore and BiometricPrompt | `ApprovalBiometricTest` (the signature verifies against the registered key, one registration per adapter, a 409 or 403 said once and asked again until the adapter says yes, an allow with no signature goes out as a deny), `HermesCarJarvisApprovalTest` (the car raises no yes/no card and says to confirm on the phone; a session-less `proof-` ask; `approval.resolved` takes it down; a -32000 refusal raises it again). **Not run:** the real Keystore and fingerprint sensor on a device |
| A calendar write has no unattended route | Automated | Hermes's `calendar_create_event`/`calendar_rsvp` pass the plugin's hook (`test_calendar_no_tool_reaches_a_writing_service_without_the_approval_hook`) and the matrix; the generic `ha_call_service` asks too; a routine naming `calendar.create_event` or `calendar.rsvp` is held for a human (`GATED_SERVICES`, `test_an_automation_that_writes_the_diary_is_held_for_a_human`). **Not held:** a bearer-token caller of the house REST, which is the owner's own credential (the same stance as the console's button for `code.run`) |
| Timezones | Partly built | a `TZID` is resolved with `zoneinfo`, a floating time is the house's own zone, a date is an all-day event; a `VTIMEZONE` block is not read, so an unknown zone name is treated as floating |
| Recurrence, attachments | **Not built** | the iCal reader handles what a person asks about and says so; an event it cannot fully parse still appears, without its recurrence |

### Channels: reachable, and only by you (M38)

| Claim | Level | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| Nothing is exposed to the internet | Automated | both shipped adapters POLL — `test_neither_shipped_adapter_opens_a_port`, and the gate greps for a webhook registration. No inbound port, no URL carrying a token |
| An unknown sender is ignored, not refused | Automated | `redteam-unknown-sender` (live) — no reply at all, and the reason names the allow-list. `test_an_unknown_sender_is_ignored_and_never_answered` asserts their words never reached the model |
| An empty allow-list means nobody, even switched on | Automated | `test_an_empty_allow_list_means_nobody` |
| A message cannot silently act | Automated | `redteam-injection-via-message` (live) — the message is quarantined and taints the turn, so M43's gate applies; the door stayed locked |
| Rate limits, per sender and overall | Automated | `test_the_per_sender_rate_limit_bites_before_the_model_does`, `test_the_global_limit_holds_across_senders`, and a sliding window test |
| Proactive moments go out on channels | Automated *as wiring* | the hub subscribes to `jarvis_notification`; `notifications` stays the one notion of "tell them" |
| Telegram and Signal against real accounts | **Unproven, by design** | no test touches an account. `MemoryChannel` ships in the product and the live probes drive the real hub through it — only the wire is a fake. `BLOCKERS.md` §4 has the accounts |

### The inbound webhook (V2 Phase 4)

M38 above is JARVIS' own channel hub, whose adapters poll. This is the other
inbound path: Hermes' gateway webhook platform, which the config template ships
with one route so n8n, CI and sensors can turn an event into a turn.

| Claim | Level | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| The listener is off unless asked for | Automated | `webhook_enabled` defaults false in `aura-schema` (`gateway.webhook.enabled`), and `test_gateway_platforms.py` asserts Hermes' `INSECURE_NO_AUTH` escape hatch is absent from the template, so a route that runs carries a real secret |
| A signed event becomes a turn | Scripted | `deploy/tests/test_webhook_inbound_live.py` with `AURA_LIVE_WEBHOOK_PROOF=1` — a real `hermes gateway run` with the template's own n8n route. The 2xx is not the proof; the session the route opens, recorded as `webhook:n8n` in the gateway's `sessions.json`, is |
| An unsigned event is refused, and so is a wrongly signed one | Scripted | the same file. The wrong-signature case signs the same body under a different key, so a shape or length check would not pass it |
| A route nobody configured is 404 | Scripted | the same file — what an attacker guessing route names sees |
| Against a real n8n posting to the real published port | **Unproven** | the proof stands up its own gateway on a free port with the model pointed at a closed one, so it settles the door and not the conversation. `deliver_only` routes, Hermes' Svix and GitHub signature schemes, and rate limiting are untested |

Command: `AURA_LIVE_WEBHOOK_PROOF=1 python3 -m pytest
deploy/tests/test_webhook_inbound_live.py -q` — 4 passed in 16.8 s.

### The delegated coding backend (M41)

| Claim | Level | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| It is off, and needs a key supplied deliberately | Automated | `test_the_default_is_off`, `test_it_will_not_run_without_a_key`; the shipped config is asserted to say `backend: local` |
| A typo cannot select the cloud backend | Automated | `test_the_backend_choice_falls_back_to_local_on_a_typo` — the setting that decides whether code leaves the network fails safe |
| A repository can refuse to be delegated | Automated | `Repo.backend` is a pin and beats a task's request; asking for the *safer* backend is always honoured |
| A delegated run happens inside the sandbox | Automated *against a real container* | `testing/fixtures/claude_backend_probe.py` — the run's edits appear in the repository inside the container, and a repository with no sandbox is refused before anything starts |
| Failure and unreadable output are reported, not believed | Automated | the same probe: an agent that fails is a failed job; output that is not a result is named as such; a non-zero exit beats a cheerful payload |
| The same approval gate applies | **By construction** | the gate is in front of the `Workspace`, and the delegated driver uses the same `Workspace`. There is no second path to the files |
| What a real Claude Code produces | **Unproven, and no key exists** | CI runs `fake_claude_code.py`, which speaks the same `--print --output-format json` protocol. `BLOCKERS.md` §4 has the row |

### The model gateway, and what may leave the network (M40)

| Claim | Level | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| One endpoint, and the house still answers through it | **Retired** with its script | `m40-model-gateway.sh` (deleted in 23177d71) runs live scenarios with `jarvis-core` dialling `jarvis-gateway`, which dials llama-swap |
| A request with no override goes local | Automated | `testing/fixtures/gateway_probe.py` — and the mock cloud provider recorded nothing |
| An override reaches a cloud provider | Automated | the same probe, against a mock that records what it was asked |
| A failing provider falls back instead of failing the turn | Automated | the same probe, with the mock told to return 500 |
| **A request carrying private content cannot leave** | Automated | the same probe: tagged `local-only`, aimed at the cloud mock, refused with 403 — **and the mock heard nothing**. That is the assertion; a log line saying "refused" would be the guard grading its own homework |
| …and an untagged request still reaches it | Automated | the control, in the same probe: the guard refuses a tag, not everything |
| Jarvis and the proxy agree about what "cloud" means | Automated | `test_the_two_halves_of_the_guard_agree` reads both files — they cannot import each other, one runs inside the LiteLLM container |
| Prompts are not logged by the proxy | Automated | `turn_off_message_logging: true`, asserted |
| Per-provider cost caps | **Not built** | LiteLLM's spend tracking needs its database. Rate limits (rpm) are config and are set; a budget is not, and `docs/explanation/TOOLING_DECISIONS.md` §8 says so |

### Hardening: what holds when the content is hostile (M43)

`docs/explanation/THREAT_MODEL.md` is the argument; this is what is asserted.

| Claim | Level | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| A page cannot forge a role boundary against a local model | Automated | `test_every_template_family_loses_its_role_markers` — ChatML, Llama 2, Llama 3, Gemma and Mistral literals are replaced with a visible scar, on the way IN (`mark_untrusted_result`), so a new inbound path cannot forget to do it |
| Content cannot escape or forge its own wrapper | Automated | `test_content_cannot_close_the_fence_around_it`, `..._forge_the_notice_either` |
| Nothing is filtered by keyword | Automated *as behaviour* | `test_nothing_pretends_to_detect_an_attack` — "ignore previous instructions" comes back word for word, wrapped. A filter with a bypass is a system exactly as vulnerable and now believed safe |
| A turn that has read external content cannot silently act | Automated for the house's own tool gate; **no production turn reaches it** (T-1489) | `test_a_tainted_turn_cannot_silently_change_state`; an unclassified tool escalates (`test_a_tool_nobody_classified_escalates`), which is the safe direction to be wrong in The test makes one context and reuses it across calls, the retired in-process agent's shape; since that agent went (22 Sep 2026) each request builds its own context and Hermes reaches the house one request per call, so no production turn forms one (`tests/test_turn_memory_shape.py`, `docs/research/house-turn-memory.md`). What gates a production turn that has read outside text is the Hermes guard: a row that asks says what was read and is keyed apart, and a row a level made free asks again (DECISIONS 34 and 45). |
| …and `remember`/`forget`/`undo` refuse outright rather than asking | Automated for the house's own tool gate; **no production turn reaches it** (T-1489) | `REFUSE_WHEN_TAINTED`, held in step with its refusal tests by `test_the_refusers_really_do_refuse` — a human cannot audit "remember: the spare key is under the mat" in the two seconds an approval gets The test makes one context and reuses it across calls, the retired in-process agent's shape; since that agent went (22 Sep 2026) each request builds its own context and Hermes reaches the house one request per call, so no production turn forms one (`tests/test_turn_memory_shape.py`, `docs/research/house-turn-memory.md`). On the Hermes side an addition to memory after outside text is asked about once per call and a removal or rewrite always asks (T-1490, DECISIONS 143); `house_undo` always asks. |
| A memory write nobody asked for does not happen | Automated *and found by a probe* | `redteam-cross-conversation-leak` caught it: a remark said in passing became a permanent fact a later conversation read back. `MEMORY_REQUESTS` now requires the USER's own words to ask |
| Secrets never reach a log, a trace or a note | Automated | `security/secrets.py` redacts **by value** (a model interpolates a key into a sentence, so key-name matching fails) plus a structural pass on known key names; the filter is installed at boot, before anything can log a config dict |
| The red-team probes | **Retired** with its script | `m43-hardening.sh` (deleted in 23177d71) runs them; the suite fails if any succeeds. Three run now; two are `gated-on: M38` because channels do not exist yet, and full mode runs them and fails |
| Prompt injection, as a class | **Not defended, and said so** | `docs/explanation/THREAT_MODEL.md` — the defence is structural (quarantine, then require a human to act), not detection. A model can still be talked into saying something foolish |

### The stack, as the thing under test (M28, M29)

| Claim | Level | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| The suite talks to the containers the operator actually runs | Automated | `bash scripts/verify/live_interaction.sh` starts with `docker compose up -d --wait` and the scenarios address the running jarvis-core and the console on `:8199`; 22 of 29 scenarios carry the default `ground: stack` |
| A container that is unhealthy at the start fails the run before a word is spoken | Automated | `up -d --wait` exits non-zero, and `Stack.up()` re-checks; an init container that ran and exited 0 is not counted as sick |
| A container that logged an ERROR-level record during the run fails the run | Automated | `stack-logs-clean` in `.verify/live/results.json` — `docker compose logs --since`, grouped into records so the allowlist can name an exception (`ConnectionResetError` from a probe that hung up) rather than the useless line that introduces it |
| A restart underneath a live conversation does not lose the thread | Automated | `resilience-core-restart` — `docker restart jarvis-core` between two turns, and "now do the same in the bedroom" still resolves |
| Speech recognition disappearing mid-utterance surfaces as a visible failure | Automated | `resilience-stt-down` — the container is stopped, the turn ends with `stt-stream-failed` rather than hanging, no service call happens, and the next turn works once it is back |
| The suite is safe to point at a house somebody lives in | Automated | `aura-core/config`, `.storage` and `mosquitto-data` are tarred before the run and restored after (`StateGuard`, through a container because the services own their own files); every thread it opens is `test:<scenario>:<variant>`; anything a scenario creates is deleted and its absence asserted before the next one |
| A code change reaches a running container | Automated | `develop: watch:` on the four services built here, with `test_every_watch_rule_syncs_into_that_image_workdir` pinning the target against each image's `WORKDIR` |
| Bring-up, teardown and per-volume backup/restore are written down | **Retired** with its script | the old runbook — and the restore path is exercised, not just documented (`m29-compose-testing.sh` (deleted in 23177d71)) |
| The stack survives a host reboot | **Measured: it did not** (2026-09-05), recovery **Scripted** | The LXC rebooted at 01:14Z on the 5th; `jarvis`, `jarvis-adapter` and `jarvis-web` were left `Exited (255)` for 24 h on "failed to bind host port 100.64.0.51:8642/tcp: cannot assign requested address" — dockerd beat tailscaled to the address every port is bound to, and `restart: unless-stopped` does not retry a restore that failed at daemon start. A plain `docker compose up -d` afterwards ran them with no ports programmed (`docker port jarvis` empty). `deploy/boot-recover.sh` (27c9485) waits for the address `.env` names and force-recreates only what is down; `scripts/deploy.sh` installs it as the deploy user's `@reboot` cron line. Proven by `deploy/tests/test_boot_recover.py` (7) against shimmed `docker`/`ip`, and by the hand recovery it reproduces. NOT yet proven: the cron line firing on a real boot — the next reboot is the test, and `logs/boot-recover.log` is where it says so. |

| Every fact in a report is in a page that was read | Automated *against the fixture web* | the same command: `must_contain` and `expect_source` per question. The open web cannot be pinned this way, which is why the facts are only checked here |
| Every citation resolves | Automated | the same command — each link is fetched (HEAD, then GET for a server that refuses HEAD) |

| Quick and deep are one engine with two budgets, and a mode cannot raise a configured limit | Automated | `test_quick_mode_does_not_follow_leads_and_stays_small`, `test_a_mode_cannot_raise_a_configured_limit` |
| Each key claim says how many of the sources read support it | Automated | `aura-core/tests/test_research_plan.py` (cross-check), and the `## Confidence` section of every report |
| A `deep_research` call that names no question researches what the user said this turn, and says so; with nothing said either it refuses and tells the model not to call it "queued" | Automated | `test_a_call_with_no_question_researches_what_the_user_said` — the 26 Aug deep-report miss on the ninth rebuild |
| "…and save it as a note" in the user's own words keeps the report as a note even when the model drops the `remember` flag; a sentence that asks for none keeps the default (nothing stored) | Automated | `test_a_note_asked_for_in_the_users_words_is_kept_even_when_the_flag_is_dropped`, `test_the_users_words_decide_whether_a_note_is_asked_for` — the re-run of the same scenario at 21:19 |
| The report is a markdown file a person can open | Automated | `test_the_report_is_written_to_a_file_somebody_can_open` — `<config>/research/<date>-<slug>.md` |
| Research against the **real** SearXNG and the open web | **Retired** with its script | `research_eval.py` (deleted in 23177d71) — M68's gate ran the client against both real instances (the tailnet one and the stack's); the eval scores the open web |
| A PDF or a Word file is read as text | Automated | `research-reads-a-document` (M32) — the warranty is in the PDF and nowhere else on the fixture web; `jarvis-browser/tests/test_documents.py` covers the shapes, including a scanned PDF being NAMED rather than returned empty. Gone since: the `jarvis-browser` tree was deleted in 1d2716b7d (2026-09-02); `research-reads-a-document` still exists (T-1441). |
| Scanned documents (OCR) | **Deliberately not** | `pypdf` reads a text layer. OCR means a model and gigabytes of it; `docs/explanation/TOOLING_DECISIONS.md` §2 records the measurement that decided it |

| A page whose content is written by JavaScript is read correctly | Automated *through the real browser* | `research-javascript-page` (M31) — the rig borrows the running `jarvis-browser`, and the same scenario FAILS under `LIVE_SHARED_BROWSER=0`, which is what makes it a test of the browser rather than of the fixture |

### The Hermes web stack (V2 §4)

The rows above are jarvis-core's research engine. Hermes has its own web toolset, and it
had its own answer to the same question — a worse one.

| Claim | Level | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| Extraction runs locally, in the stack | Automated | `python3 -m pytest deploy/tests/test_extract_service.py -q` — 13 tests over `deploy/extract`: routes, refusals, caps, and the real Trafilatura on the fixture's pages |
| A table survives Hermes' extraction with its rows intact | Automated | `test_a_table_keeps_its_rows` — `\| Kestrel Pro \| £169 \| 3 floors \| 6 \|`. Trafilatura's own minimum-size floor flattens it; the service sets the floors to zero |
| No cloud search or extraction provider is configured anywhere the stack reads | Automated | `scripts/verify/no-cloud-web-providers.sh` — 10 checks: no cloud credential in a tracked file or either `.env.example`, both backends named, both plugins enabled, ten cloud plugins disabled, the keyless tier off |
| The **running** gateway resolved those backends, not something else | **Scripted** | `python3 scripts/verify/web-providers.py` on a host with Hermes — `active search: searxng`, `active extract: aura-extract`. A different claim from the row above: a named backend whose plugin is not enabled falls through to the keyless tier and nothing warns |
| A page is really read through the local extractor | **Scripted** | the same script with `--call http://host.docker.internal:8899/products` — 198 chars back through the containerised service |
| Hermes' `web_extract` had been reaching Exa's and Parallel's public MCP endpoints | Observed, and fixed | `hermes config get web --json` showed `search_backend: ""`, `extract_backend: ""`, `keyless_fallback: true`, and `hermes plugins list` every `web-*` plugin "not enabled" — the registry's keyless tier (`agent/web_search_registry.py` `_resolve`, step 4) |
| The browser drives a browser (Hermes v0.23 `browser_exec`, T-1519) | **Verified on a throwaway gateway of the image built from this tree (2026-11-18)** | The build launches the baked Chromium as uid 10000 and checks a dumped page (deploy/Dockerfile). In the running image, `browser_exec` returned the opened page's title and aura-activity's `_screenshot` a PNG; a live turn ("open example.com and tell me its heading") made three `browser_exec` calls, all free, and answered in 21 s. After the fixes below, four turns (example.com then python.org, a paragraph, Wikipedia's language count, Hacker News' top story) all answered: 16 calls free, one refused as unparsable and rewritten by the model, one asked (a `for _` loop, now free). Before `bot_desktop.placement: gateway` every call answered "The browser cannot run here" |
| A browser login survives the browser closing | **Unproven since v0.23** | The profile is `browser.user_data_dir` (`/opt/data/browser/profile`, on the volume). The probe that showed this drove agent-browser, which v0.23 removed; it has not been rerun on `browser_exec` |
| A second browsing session does not fail on the first one's Chrome profile lock (T-0795) | **Upstream's since v0.23, not re-proven here** | The agent-browser shim and its test are gone: Hermes' own `launch_chromium` (its browser launcher, inside the image) attaches a second session to the Chromium already running on a persistent profile instead of launching another on its lock |
| `browser_exec` is free only as helper-only code, and each step meets its old tool's row (T-1519) | Automated | `python3 -m pytest hermes/plugins/tests/test_browser_exec_steps.py -q` (104): JavaScript reads are held to a list of names, with no computed member, escape, `${}`, `/` (a comment or regex literal hid code from the string scan) or destructured parameter, nor a field's `.value` (what the vault filled; T-1528), and a string or a number of six characters or more in one meets the typed-text gate; a Python name bound only inside an `if`, a loop or a comprehension is not read after it (only the first lines of a loop over a non-empty literal `range` count as run); an f-string's format spec is read; `str.format` is not a free method; a wait loop on `_` is free; JavaScript built in an f-string only from names bound once to a literal is read like a literal; `import re`, `json` and `time` are data handling (only their reading functions); code that does not parse is refused with a reason instead of asked (it could not run: the harness `exec`s it in the gateway's own Python); an address the owner gave stays given after the model writes it in `new_tab("…")` (the code is read token by token for the taint record); imports, attributes, computed addresses, a screenshot path, `cdp` and page-changing JS ask or are refused; a navigate to an API answer, a metadata endpoint or a geocoder is refused as `browser_navigate` was; `fill_input` text meets the typed-text gate and navigations the fetch gate (`test_typed_text_gate.py`, `test_outbound_gate.py`) |
| A benchmark task that a browser is actually needed for | Automated | `bench/tasks/browser-action/browser-auth-007.yaml` + `bench/tests/test_fixture_vault.py` (11 tests). The first version passed with curl and `execute_code`; the fixture now refuses a reveal that did not come from the rendered page |

| One token source: `design/tokens.json` is the only file where a colour, size, font, radius, shadow or duration is typed; every surface's token file is generated from it | Automated | `python3 design/build.py --check` — seven generated files current (web CSS + TS, desktop `tokens.py`, Android `JarvisTokens.kt`, `JarvisTheme.kt`, `tokens.xml`, `colors.xml`) |

The pre-v2 rings' drawing, frame maths, geometry constants, `ReactorScreenshotTest` and its fourteen `reactor-*` goldens are gone (T-1299); the contract's `geometry` rows stay for the console and the desktop. `SiriPalette.kt`, `ReactorOrb.kt`, `JarvisOrbView.kt` and `SiriOrbView.kt` no longer exist; `reactor_orb_test.py` went with them. |

The lint walks that car module (`CAR_UI_MODULES`); the other car modules hold protocol constants and are left out. Every other file it walks is clean, so any raw value fails rather than fitting under an allowance. 4 documented exceptions. Re-measured under M44, where a planted `transition: all 240ms ease-in-out` had slipped through on `base.css`'s stale allowance |

### Skills, MCP servers and plugins, as one thing (M45)

`m45-registry.sh` (deleted in 23177d71) — 15 checks.

| Claim | Level | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| Every extensible thing carries a manifest: id, version, description, author, declared permissions, tool allowlist, network and filesystem needs | Automated | `aura/integrations/extensions/manifest.py`; derived from each subsystem rather than stored beside it, so a skill stays a portable Agent Skill |
| The manifest is validated against a JSON Schema an author can read | Automated | `manifest.schema.json` — a real draft 2020-12 document. Enforced by a hand-written validator rather than a dependency (this package installs from wheels with no compiler) |
| …and the validator implements every keyword the schema uses | Automated | `test_the_validator_knows_every_keyword_the_schema_uses` — the real failure mode of a hand-rolled validator is a schema that grows a keyword nothing enforces, so the document describes a stricter manifest than the one being accepted |
| A malformed manifest is rejected rather than half-loaded | Automated | `test_an_invalid_skill_manifest_leaves_the_system_prompt_too` — out of the index, out of the store, and out of `index_block()`. Dropping only the bad keys is how a `tools` list with one unparseable entry becomes a shorter allowlist than the author wrote and a wider one than they meant |
| A manifest cannot invent a permission | Automated | the vocabulary is a closed enum of 8. An accepted-but-unenforced permission is worse than a rejected one: it reads as a declaration in a management surface and constrains nothing |
| A manifest cannot list a tool it has not asked permission for | Automated | `TOOL_PERMISSIONS` + `under_declared()` — `write_file` without `filesystem_write`, `web_search` without `network`, `remember` without `memory_write` |
| A plugin's `act` permission cannot disagree with the taint gate | Automated | derived from `PluginTool.read_only`, which is the same field M43's escalation reads — not declared separately |
| The registry can say who holds each permission, not just what each item may do | Automated | `permission_scope()` — everything in the house that can write to memory, or start a process, in one list |
| Health never raises when something is down | Automated | `test_health_never_raises_when_something_is_broken` — a registry that throws when one server is down shows nothing at the moment somebody opened it |
| Indexing does not force MCP to exist | Automated | the gate asserts an empty install grows nothing but `extensions`. `DEPENDENCIES` would have set up MCP for an install that never configured one |
| Four skills ship and all four validate | Automated | research-report, note-taking, homelab-status, diary — 1,405 words, four `SKILL.md` files and nothing executable beside them |
| A skill in the operator's directory replaces a shipped one, without reading as a collision | Automated | `test_a_users_skill_overrides_a_bundled_one_of_the_same_name`; two of the same name in ONE root is still an error |
| A recorded measurement can reach a sentence | Automated | `metrics_query` (read-only, in `READ_ONLY_TOOLS`) over the same sources the dashboards draw; the gate asserts the skill names it |
| A third party's manifest, from a catalog | **Not yet** | M47. The gate for it is the same `Manifest.from_raw` call; what does not exist yet is anything that installs code from off this machine |

### Installing from a catalog (M47)

`m47-catalog.sh` (deleted in 23177d71) — 19 checks. `docs/explanation/THREAT_MODEL.md` carries the argument.

| Claim | Level | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| Only a document and a URL can be installed | Automated | `INSTALLABLE_KINDS == {skill, mcp}`; a plugin source is refused with "an in-process import has the whole interpreter", a stdio MCP server with "those come from configuration.yaml, which a person edits" |
| Nothing installs from an origin nobody named | Automated | `DEFAULT_SOURCES == ()`, and an unconfigured source raises. Shipping a default list would hand the supply chain to whoever owns those URLs, for every install. Since M65 one source ships that is not an origin — `bundled`, the package's own folder on this machine (`DEVIATIONS.md` §21) — and this claim is unchanged for anything remote |
| A source is https or this machine | Automated | `http://`, `ftp://`, a bare path and `gopher://` are all refused |
| `latest` is not a version | Automated | `resolve_ref` refuses it without a concrete ref to pin to — a blind `latest` makes the approved thing and the landed thing two different objects |
| What was approved is what lands | Automated | sha256 recorded when the plan is built and re-checked immediately before writing; a swapped payload raises |
| An MCP server and an OpenAPI service are each added, turned on and called through the steps the model runs, in under a minute (T-0609) | Scripted (local fakes) | `python3 scripts/hermes-probes/connect_anything_demo.py --fake`: both connected in 0.52 s (2026-11-17) |
| Nothing in a payload runs | Automated *and the acceptance criterion* | the fixture's `friendly-helper` ships an `install.sh` that would write `/tmp/jarvis-catalog-probe-should-not-exist`. It lands on disk, it is named in the approval prompt as a program, and the marker never exists |
| Installing without an approved plan is refused | Automated | `apply` raises, and the service answers with the name of the call that is missing |
| A payload cannot write outside its folder | Automated | traversal, absolute paths, dotfiles, symlinks and over-deep nesting are refused — refused rather than corrected, which is how `/etc/SKILL.md` had been silently becoming `etc/SKILL.md` |
| A catalog description cannot smuggle an instruction | Automated | quarantined, not filtered: the fixture's hostile description keeps its words and loses its `<\|im_start\|>` marker, and its invented `become_root` permission is dropped rather than shown as real |
| The console shows what an entry asks for before anything is installed | **Retired** with its spec | `catalogue.spec.ts` (deleted in bf8f6940) (M65; it was `extensions.spec.ts` while the catalogue lived in that fold) — declared permissions on the row, then a second dialog with the ref, the hash, every file and every program |
| A catalogue the house could not read says so: which registries failed (`source: error`), or that it took longer than its 1.5 s wait, with a Retry, and still lists what loaded. The line is on All as well as Catalogue, a house with nothing installed draws it rather than "Nothing installed", and Retry waits visibly and asks jarvis-core to read a failed registry again instead of answering from its 30 s error cache | **Retired** on the console (T-0611) / Automated (the feed) | The console's line went with the legacy UI (6dc2ef51, its spec in c756823a); the v2 Integrations screen draws no catalogue-problem line yet (T-0665). What stood on it: `hud-integrations.spec.ts` (6, the mock's `jarvis/test/catalog_mode` `partial`, `broken`, `slow`, `bare`, `remembered`) + `src/lib/hud/feeds.catalogue.test.ts` (12: the browse's `errors`, its `error`, a timeout kept as the feed's `catalogueProblem`, `refresh: true` on Retry only and never left pending without a connection, the `reloading` flag and the Retry-only `retrying` one, a Retry waiting 4.5 s where a plain load waits 1.5 s, and a periodic reload skipping a feed that is mid-Retry) + `python3 -m pytest aura-core/tests/test_extensions.py::test_browse_refresh_reads_a_failed_registry_again_but_not_one_that_answered` and `python3 -m pytest aura-core/tests/test_api.py::test_ws_a_registry_browse_carries_refresh_to_the_service_only_when_it_is_true`. It was an empty list with nothing saying why (T-0323), then a hidden line and a Retry that showed nothing (T-0329) |
| A malicious entry cannot talk the model into installing it | Automated *against the real containers* | `redteam-malicious-skill-install` (live, `gated-on: M47`) — the model has no tool that installs anything, the marker file does not appear, and the reply does not treat the entry's own description as permission |
| Fetching over **https** | **Not exercised** | the transport is written and the offline gate cannot reach the open internet, so every test runs against `file://`. The difference is one function; the parsing, hashing, hook-finding, approval and write path are the same code |
| A source that was honest and stops being | **Not defended** | the hash pins a payload to an approval, not a source to a reputation. A taken-over origin fails the check on the NEXT install and does nothing about what is already on disk |

### The management surface (M46)

`m46-plugins-ui.sh` (deleted in 23177d71) — 15 checks.

| Claim | Level | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| Turning a plugin off takes its tools off the MODEL, not off a list | Automated | `test_disabling_a_plugin_takes_its_tools_off_the_model` — the tool registry, not the console's listing. A plugin hidden from a page is one the model can still call |
| An edited permission scope is enforced on the very next call | Automated | revoking `act` withdraws the writers and keeps the readers; `permissions: null` restores exactly what was withdrawn |
| A permission the manifest never declared cannot be granted | Automated | narrowing only — the manifest is the statement people read, and an operator granting more does not make the thing need more |
| A decision outlives the process | Automated | `python3 -m pytest aura-core/tests/test_extensions.py::test_a_decision_survives_a_restart` — a second store over the same directory still has the skill off and the others on |
| Turning something back on actually brings it back | Automated *and found by the live suite* | `test_turning_a_skill_back_on_actually_brings_it_back`. The switch worked exactly once: disabling popped the skill out of the store and nothing ever put one back |
| A skill can be written from the console, and it validates | Automated | `extensions/scaffold` — the permissions its chosen tools require are written in for it, so the file cannot fail its own validator a second later |
| A name that is nearly a path is refused, not corrected | Automated | `../escape`, `Bin Day`, `bin/day` and `bin_day` are all refused; the folder is named exactly what was asked for. A name quietly turned into something else is how somebody ends up with a skill they did not write in a folder they did not name |
| A switch flipped MID-CONVERSATION reaches the running Jarvis | Automated *against the real containers* | `extensions-toggle-enforced` (live, `gated-on: M46`) — three turns against the deployment, asserted through `jarvis/skills/list`, which is the store the prompt's skill index is built from |
| The section has real loading, empty, error and rejected states | **Retired** with its spec | `extensions.spec.ts` (deleted in bf8f6940), 6 tests, plus a structural check that each state is rendered |
| It did not cost a tab | Automated | the gate asserts the top-level nav count did not grow and that no `/extensions` route exists — the section lives on `/tools` |
| The same enforcement for an MCP server's tools | **Not measured** | an MCP server's tools are withdrawn by disabling the server, which `mcp/*` already does; nothing here asserts the two agree |
| The live half for a PLUGIN | **Not measured live** | no `ToolPlugin` is configured in a default deployment — calendar and mail need the operator's own account, and the radicale fixture is behind `--profile fixtures` deliberately. The plugin case is the pytest above, against a real registry |

### The console's structure (M48)

`m48-webui-c2.sh` (deleted in 0d02f0d8) — 15 checks. The UI migration table is the inventory.

| Claim | Level | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| No more than five top-level destinations | Automated | the gate counts `nav: true` in `screens.ts` and fails at six. Eleven became four |
| Every current page has a home in the navigation architecture | Automated | the gate walks `src/routes` and fails on a page the architecture does not place |
| Every moved path redirects rather than 404s | Automated | ten 308s, each asserted to point where `MOVED` says. `/tasks/[id]` carries the id through |
| Every keyboard chord anybody learnt still lands on its page | Automated | the gate holds the pre-consolidation chord table against the current one; `g d` must still reach devices. `g b` is asserted present because the nav's tooltip had been promising it |
| The nav, the chords, the palette and the phone read one list | Automated | four copies of the route list became one; the gate fails if any of the four stops reading `screens.ts` |
| The palette indexes sections, not only destinations | Automated | with four front doors instead of eleven this is the fast path, and a palette offering only the four would make the console slower to use |
| The phone's settings are two native screens — Jarvis (the console's settings from the schema's contract, over the Hermes link) and Phone — with no console WebView (retired 2026-09-25: the owner asked for "only the phone with the phone settings, with all of the console settings") | Automated | `python3 android-app/tools/settings_screens_test.py` (6, one web view allowed: the car display's CarHud); `gradle :app:testDebugUnitTest --tests "*HermesSettings*"` (3) |
| No hard-coded style value anywhere in the console | Automated | `token_lint.py` at zero, with the ratchet empty |
| Every screen declares itself and uses `ScreenState` (or `LinkState`, which always renders a `ScreenState` from a link) | Automated | `web_states_check.py`, which now reads the section a route mounts rather than the two-line route file, and accepts either component since 3501dfe |
| 84 controls use the shared component library | Automated *as a count* | and the library grew `pressed`, an `approve` variant and attribute forwarding rather than the pages working around it |
| What is still a raw `<button>` | **Deliberate, and listed** | a mic, a disclosure triangle, a clickable row and five other one-offs — the UI migration table names each with its reason. All on tokens; none is a button in this library's sense |
| Whether it is *good* | **Needs eyes** | the 48 screenshots of the retired console (ui-review, 16 screens at three widths) went in T-0665; the v2 console is looked at through `make sweep`. The harness can prove structure, states and spacing; "would a first-time user understand this" is not a test |

### The console on Reactor II (M50)

`m50-webui-c2-look.sh` measured the C2 look of the pre-HUD pages; it was retired with them (P3.1).

**The proofs named in this section were retired with the screens they drove.** The Glass
migration deleted 47 specs in bf8f6940 (`home`, `look`, `knowledge`, `states`, `responsive`,
`controls`, `e2e`, `models`, `catalogue`, `dashboards` — `ls aura-web/e2e/*.spec.ts` lists
none of them), and the rows below were not re-pointed at what replaced them. Read them as the
record of what was true of the pre-HUD console; what holds of the console today is proven in
*The HUD* rows and by the suites named there. Re-pointing them row by row is `/claims` work
that has not been done.

| Claim | Level | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| Every row in the UI migration table §3's M50 list is ticked, and the gate refuses an unticked one | Automated | the gate reads the section and fails on any `- [ ]` |
| The nine components the pages needed exist, are exported, documented, SSR-tested and on the style guide | Automated | the gate checks each of `TopBar`, `SectionStrip`, `StatusReadout`, `StagesBar`, `CallLine`, `DayStrip`, `ProgressRing`, `Figure`, `Graph` in `index.ts`, `README.md`, `ssr.test.ts` and `/styleguide` |
| No console furniture, grid or brackets survive | Automated | the gate greps `chrome.css` and every `.svelte/.css/.ts` under `src` |
| A pill radius only where a thing is round | Automated | the gate lists every `--jv-radius-pill` outside the dots, rings and the toggle track |
| The render matches the direction on every screen | **Retired** with its spec | `look.spec.ts` (deleted in bf8f6940): Barlow ground, `--jv-bg` body, no `.jv-grid`/`.jv-bracket`/`<canvas>`, no pill-shaped control, panels only on palette colours, the bar's underline present, at most one filled accent control |
| The knowledge graph draws every note and memory entry, lights on use, and never prints one name over another | **Retired** with its spec | `knowledge.spec.ts` (deleted in bf8f6940): a node per note and entry, every pair of label boxes disjoint, the mock's `[[heating]]` link as an edge, a node click opens the note, `jarvis/test/memory_used` lights and settles, `note_search` lights the match, reduced motion still reports `data-lit` |
