## Safety and governance (v2 Phase 7, 2026-09-03)

The distinction this section turns on, and the reason it is split in two: the
tests prove the approval matrix **decides** correctly. They cannot prove it is
**consulted** on the JARVIS host. Those are different claims and only the first
one is Automated today.

`aura-guard` — the plugin that registers the one `pre_tool_call` hook — is not
in the running image (`jarvis:64c15083`, built 13:07 on 2026-09-03) and not in
the live `plugins.enabled`. Until a pipeline deploy carries it, **every row in
the second table below is Unproven against this deployment**, including the ones
whose logic is exhaustively tested. A table that decides correctly and is never
asked is not a control.

### What the tests prove

| Claim | Level | Proof / command |
|---|---|---|
| The matrix gives the right verdict for every classified tool and argument shape | Automated | `python3 -m pytest hermes/plugins/tests/test_matrix.py -q` — the two baseline failures (`terminal` deleting a folder, `ha_call_service` unlocking a door) are the first two tests |
| Every tool the plugins register has a row; no row names a tool nothing registers | Automated | `test_the_matrix_covers_every_tool_the_deployment_registers`, `test_no_row_promises_to_govern_a_tool_that_does_not_exist` — both load every plugin through `FakeCtx` |
| An unclassified tool asks rather than runs | Automated | `test_an_unclassified_tool_asks_rather_than_runs` |
| A plugin's own gate agrees with the matrix row for the same tool | Automated | `test_every_tool_a_plugin_gates_is_gated_by_the_matrix_too` — two gates that disagree resolve by hook registration order, which is not a policy |
| The gate turns a verdict into the directive Hermes expects, and the approval reaches a human carrying the tool name | Automated | `test_our_approve_directive_reaches_a_human_carrying_the_tool_name` — drives Hermes' own `request_tool_approval` and asserts on the event a gateway notify callback receives |
| An approved destructive action and a refused one land in the audit log distinguishably | Automated | `python3 -m pytest hermes/plugins/aura-guard -q` |
| A secret in a tool result, a log line and the assistant's reply comes out redacted, in the shapes this deployment issues | Automated | `test_the_same_secret_is_masked_on_all_three_surfaces`, `test_the_two_shapes_hermes_does_not_know_are_the_ones_the_registry_adds` — the second runs Hermes unregistered in a subprocess, so it fails if the registration stops taking effect |
| The kill switch stops everything that is not a pure read, and the model cannot release it | Automated | `test_a_free_write_does_not_survive_the_switch`, `test_the_model_cannot_release_the_switch` |
| One approval card for a multi-step plan: an approved `plan_approve` lets exactly its listed calls (tool + identical arguments) through, once each, for that session, for ten minutes; a target smuggled into another argument, other arguments, another session, a lapsed plan, a guest, the kill switch and DENY rows are unaffected, and each card's rule_key is unique so a remembered "always" never approves a later plan | Automated | `python3 -m pytest hermes/plugins/tests/test_plan.py -q` (13), through the real `matrix_gate` and the guard's handler |
| The console's approval card names its risk (Low risk / Can't be undone / Confirm on phone), offers "Ask why" (a no, then the question said to the assistant; never a yes), and a queue of two or more undoable, non-phone approvals gets "Approve all N" for exactly the ids drawn; at 390×844 the common card fits the screen with no scrolling, the "Next time" rule folded behind "Show me first" (T-0694). Does not cover a third risk level. The phone's native approval screen offers the same "Ask why" on a Hermes approval only (never its own Tier-3 asks): a deny on the wire, then the contract's line (`tests/contracts/approval-event.json` `ask_why`) as one `prompt.submit`, held until the running turn ends and dropped when the link closes, the session changes or 60 s pass; the countdown's auto-deny never asks. **Never run on a real phone** (T-1054) | Automated | `cd aura-web && npx vitest run src/lib/ds/shell` (81 tests) and `npx playwright test e2e/v2-shell.spec.ts -g approval`; the phone: `gradle -p android-app :app:testDebugUnitTest --tests '*Approval*'` and `python3 -m pytest android-app/tools -k approval` |
| Every tool the deployed Hermes exposes has an approval-matrix row, and a built-in Hermes renamed keeps its old row (cronjob_manage, todo_list, process_manage -- including process's DENY rows) | Automated + Containerised | `python3 -m pytest hermes/plugins/tests/test_matrix.py -q`; the deploy smoke's step 1b runs the inventory inside the image, where Hermes is importable (it printed the nine unknowns against the 2026-10-06 production image) |
| A homelab service that failed three times in a row on connection-shaped errors is refused for a minute with a message naming it; an answer the service gave (404, 401, bad arguments) never opens it; one success closes it | Automated | `python3 -m pytest hermes/plugins/tests/test_breaker.py -q` (11), through the real `matrix_gate` and `audit_outcome_hook` |
| Both readers of the kill-switch file agree, including on the failure directions | Automated | `cd aura-adapter && python3 -m pytest tests/test_kill_switch.py -q` against `tests/contracts/kill-switch.json` |
| The adapter can actually reach the file in production | Automated *(found by the rollback drill)* | It could not, from 7c419715 to 6e0bea73: Hermes keeps its home 0700, the adapter is another uid, and "unreadable" reads as ENGAGED — so `GET /api/status` said engaged on a stack with no switch file and the HUD showed the kill state. `HERMES_HOME_MODE=0701` in `deploy/docker-compose.yml` (`python3 -m pytest deploy/tests/test_pipeline.py::test_the_adapter_can_reach_the_kill_switch_through_hermes_home`) and 0644 on the record (`test_the_record_is_readable_by_the_adapter_whatever_the_umask`). The live reading after the deploy of 6e0bea73 is in the September 2026 build log's FINAL REPORT. The setting alone did not hold: Hermes applies it when a process starts and sets the folder back to 0700 whenever it saves its credential store, twelve seconds after the first start of a new install, so a new install's console drew the kill state from sign-in on (the clean-install proof at bd4969950c51, red twice, 2026-11-17). Since T-1497 the gateway holds the mode: a keeper started by `aura-guard` looks once a second and puts the set mode back (`python3 -m pytest hermes/plugins/aura-guard/tests/test_home_mode.py -q`, 24 tests; on the proof's machine the store was written at 04:03:54.8 and the mode was back at 04:03:55.5). Not covered: a status request inside that second still reads the switch as engaged, once. |
| The coding sandbox's container arguments still isolate it, and one host path is mounted | Automated | `bash scripts/egress-audit.sh` (checks 1–2); `cd aura-core && python3 -m pytest tests/test_code_sandbox.py -q` |
| The egress allowlist refuses an undeclared upload and a metadata endpoint, and leaves reads alone | Automated | `bash scripts/egress-audit.sh` (checks 3–4) |
| CI fails when plugin code names a public host that is not declared: every http(s) host in a string constant outside tests/ (docstrings and comments skipped, userinfo seen through) must be in `_egress.PLUGIN_HOSTS` with its reason, and a declared host no plugin names any more fails too; the five today are open-meteo's two (weather), Photon's and Nominatim's (off by default) and claude.ai (a trailer's text, never fetched). The Ops board's Activity pane asks **Sent today** for the audit log's outbound rows (T-0573). Not covered: a host built at runtime from a setting (the derived allowlist), jarvis-core, the adapter and the apps (deploy/tests/test_location_egress.py covers the location services there) | Automated | `python3 -m pytest hermes/plugins/tests/test_egress_hosts.py -q` (3; red before PLUGIN_HOSTS, and names `aura-hud/weather.py:29 api.open-meteo.com` with one entry removed); `E2E_PORT=8320 npx playwright test e2e/v2-ops.spec.ts` |

### What is not proven on the deployed stack

| Claim | Level | Why not, and what would prove it |
|---|---|---|
| The matrix is consulted at all on the JARVIS host | **Unproven** | `aura-guard` is not in the image or in `plugins.enabled`. A pipeline deploy, then `bench run --category safety` against the stack. |
| The kill switch stops a production turn | **Unproven** *(drilled in isolation)* | Drilled end to end on an isolated gateway with the plugin loaded: engaged from the host, the next write was refused and the file did not appear; the model reported it had no path to release it; released from the host, the same request succeeded. Nothing has done that against production. |
| The kill switch stops a run that is **already in flight** | Scripted *against a live gateway* | `scripts/hermes-probes/kill-switch-mid-action.py`, run 2026-09-03 against an isolated `hermes serve`. Engaged from the host 5.2 s into a four-step job: the in-flight `terminal` call finished (the brake stops the next action, not the executing one), then the same write was refused twice — once as `terminal`, once as `write_file`, both `rule_key=kill-switch` — one of three files was created, and reads kept answering. The verdict is read from the audit log, not the transcript, because a model that stops and a model that was stopped read the same. Not run against production. |
| Both governance tools answer when a gateway calls them | Automated | `test_every_tool_handler_takes_the_kwargs_the_registry_passes` over all 21 plugins. Until 2026-09-03 `kill_switch` and `audit_query` raised `TypeError: … unexpected keyword argument 'task_id'` on every gateway call, and 51 green tests said otherwise because `FakeCtx` called handlers with one positional argument. The fake now dispatches the way `ToolRegistry.dispatch` does. |
| The audit log exists on the host | **Unproven** | It is written by the plugin. No plugin, no log — which is also why the `audit_gated` benchmark checker fails loudly rather than passing when the file is absent. |
| Secret redaction on the host covers the Tailscale and Proxmox shapes | **Unproven** | The patterns are registered by the plugin at load. Hermes v0.21.0's own redactor returns both unchanged, so until the deploy those two shapes are unmasked in production tool output and logs. |
| Sandbox network isolation as deployed | Scripted *against a real container* | `bash scripts/egress-audit.sh --live`, run here 2026-09-03: 7 passed, 1 failed. The failure is not the sandbox — `extract:8080/health` is unreachable because no `jarvis-extract` container exists on this host. Section 6 starts a container from `container_argv()`'s own argv and finds five destinations unreachable under `--network=none`, with the `network=egress` pass reaching 1.1.1.1 as the positive control. Under egress the host and the LAN also time out here, which is this host's ufw policy rather than the sandbox — `sandbox.py`'s docstring still warns that egress reaches the LAN, and that conservative claim is deliberately unchanged. |
| The safety benchmark category with the gate live | Scripted | 100 % (8/8) in the full run against 17de5241 on 2026-09-06 (`bench/results/final-17de5241.md`; the gate plugin loaded, six of the eight on the gateway route where the harness answers the approval). Overall 63/86, 73.3 %. |
| Backup restore drill | **Unproven** | §8 of V2_PLAN lists it; nothing here has restored the Hermes volume. |
| TIRITH guardrails | **Unproven** | Present in the install (`~/.hermes/bin/tirith`); not enabled or evaluated. |

One correction to an older row while I was here: **Sandbox network isolation as
deployed** above pointed at `./scripts/egress-audit.sh`, which had been deleted
in Phase G along with the service it audited. The Makefile kept a `.PHONY:
egress-audit` line with no recipe under it, so `make egress-audit` exited 0 by
doing nothing while this document listed it among the commands that close the
hardware-backed gaps. The script is back, pointed at where the fences live now,
and the recipe runs it.

## Proactivity, documents and images (v2 Phase 6, 2026-09-03)

Three capabilities that all failed the baseline for the same structural reason: the
thing that was missing was never the code, it was what reached the deployed image.
The running stack is `jarvis:64c15083`, whose seed carries two of the repo's
nineteen skills. **Every row marked "not on this deployment" below is proven in the
tree and unproven in production until a pipeline deploy carries it**, which is the
distinction this section exists to keep visible.

### Scheduled work reaching a person

| Claim | Level | Proof / command |
|---|---|---|
| A watchdog-shaped job (`--script … --no-agent`, no model) delivers its stdout to a messaging platform | Automated (live, opt-in) | `AURA_LIVE_CRON_PROOF=1 python3 -m pytest deploy/tests/test_cron_delivery_live.py -q` — a real `hermes gateway run`, the real Telegram adapter, the real scheduler; only the Bot API host is substituted, through PTB's own `base_url` |
| A brief-shaped job (a real agent turn against the resident model) delivers its answer the same way | Automated (live, opt-in) | same file, same test — both shapes in one gateway, because the agentless path never touches the model client and the agent path never touches the script runner |
| Moving every shipped job to a platform is one environment value | Automated | `test_one_environment_value_moves_every_job_to_a_platform` — `PROACTIVE_DELIVER=telegram` and the brief, the watchdog and the hourly health check all come out with `--deliver telegram`, and the brief stops pushing through `house_tell_user` |
| A bot token alone is **not** a delivery target: a bare `telegram` needs `TELEGRAM_HOME_CHANNEL`, and without it the job reports `ok` and tells nobody | Automated (live, opt-in) | `test_a_job_with_no_home_channel_delivers_nowhere` — judged only after a control job with an explicit `telegram:<chat_id>` has landed in the same gateway, so the negative cannot pass by being early |
| `hermes cron list` says so, on a line below the execution line | Automated (live, opt-in) | the same test asserts `Delivery failed: no delivery target resolved`; `docs/runbooks/proactive.md` quotes the three lines and warns that `grep -A3` cuts the warning off |
| Delivery to a real Telegram or Discord account | **Unproven, needs the owner** | no bot token exists in this repo. The five lines that close it are in `docs/runbooks/messaging.md`; only the token and the owner's numeric id cannot be written here |
| The GPU watchdog reads real telemetry | Containerised — 2026-09-03 21:56Z: both 3090s at 54 °C / 45 °C and 89.9 % VRAM, under every default limit, so it correctly says nothing | `handle_gpu_status` against the deployed container's Grafana/InfluxDB |
| An install with no GPU telemetry gets no GPU watchdog and no "check Grafana" notice (T-1502, 2026-11-17): the routine's line in `hermes/cron/jobs.tsv` names `GRAFANA_URL|INFLUX_URL` in a `needs` column, and the seeder creates it only where one is set, removes one it seeded earlier when both are gone (and forgets it, so a later job of that name is not taken back), and leaves a job of that name it did not create. The watch itself prints nothing and writes no state with neither set; with one set and unreachable it still says so once | Automated. Not covered: a source set in the settings after the start reaches the routine list at the next restart, not before; a source that is set and wrong is an outage the watch reports, not a missing source; no other shipped routine names a need yet | `python3 -m pytest deploy/tests/test_cron_seed.py hermes/plugins/gpu-telemetry/tests/test_watch.py -q` (49 passed); on production, where one source is set, `docker exec jarvis grep -c 'GPU temperature watchdog' /opt/data/cron/jobs.json` prints 1 |
| Its debounce rules (edge-trigger, hysteresis, hourly ceiling, quiet hours) | Automated | `python3 -m pytest hermes/plugins/gpu-telemetry/tests -q` |
| The brief and the watchdog are seeded by the image on every start, and adopt or retire what an older image left | Automated | `python3 -m pytest deploy/tests/test_cron_seed.py -q` — twelve tests against a temp `HERMES_HOME` with a recording stand-in for the `hermes` CLI |

| Music asked for "on my phone" from a conversation the phone is not in reaches the phone (T-1474): `phone_media` sent `hud.phone.media` to the asking session only, so at the desk console the desk's own session took the frame, nothing there acts on it, and the reply read `ok`, `delivered`. When the adapter's surface record says the conversation is no phone's, the request now goes over the house's device channel to the one phone on it: the media keys (`media_control`) for pause, next, previous and a bare play, and the phone's new `media_search` action (handed to the handler the phone's own conversation uses) for anything named, queued or shuffled. With no phone reached the reply is `ok: false` and says so, and the desk's session is sent nothing. In the phone's own conversation it is the session's frame as before; when nobody can say whose conversation it is, the session is tried first and the house only if it took nothing. Only the owner's request takes the house's channel: a guest's or a member's never opens the owner's phone. | T-0979 — The house keeps map regions as packs and answers the car's ground from them: `pack.py` builds one SQLite file per region (footprints, roads with one-way and speed, places, indexed by 0.01 degree cell; `tests/contracts/map-pack.json`), `/api/maps/buildings` answers from the packs covering the bbox and falls back to the home lists, and a pack's answer for a box equals the lists' answer for it. The owner (or AURA: `map_regions`, `map_region_download`, `map_region_remove`, the last one asked) asks for any Geofabrik region; the house writes the ask and `scripts/map_worker.py` (`maps-worker`) fetches the extract, builds the pack and hands the extract to Valhalla. An owner's `car_navigate` to somewhere no pack covers asks for that region (`maps.auto_fetch`); a guest's never does, and the privacy switch stops it and the weekly catalogue refresh. Measured on this host (11 Nov 2026): the home 50 km box (250,186 footprints, 112,830 roads, 77,259 places) packs to 116.6 MB and answers a 0.01 degree box in 6 ms; the worker fetched and packed Monaco from Geofabrik in 3 s in a scratch directory. Headway's stack now builds inside the Android project. Its tiers (`docs/car/PROGRESS.md`) map
onto these levels as: A (executed in CI) → **Automated** here only once the suite has run in
*this* repo; B (run on a device) and D (needs the car) → **Unproven** for Jarvis until a Jarvis
build repeats them, because what was observed was the Headway APK, not this one. Nothing below
is promoted by the move alone.

| A failed connection gate maps to one remedy; a car's profile round-trips and never crosses cars; a car-opened socket waits for its own car only | Automated | `*GateReport*` (9), `*CarProfile*` (5), `*PokeSlot*` (4) |
| The car's microphone goes to Jarvis's transcriber; the grammar acts first; a question is answered through the car; an unanswered approval is denied; a cancelled turn is never spoken; a blank transcript is never a question; the Jarvis tab's reactor follows the car's turn (listening, thinking, speaking, a refused voice, offline) | Automated | `*JarvisTranscriber*` (4), `*CarTurn*` (7), `*CarVoice*` (4), `*ApprovalChoice*` (3) |

| "Hey Jarvis" is heard on the phone, not by streaming the microphone to the server: on-device detection is the default and the listener fetches its three ONNX files through the adapter (`/api/models/<name>.onnx`, proxied to the house; the adapter answered 404 before, so the phone had streamed 32 KB/s all day); GPS for place routines at most every 5 min, the 20 s protocol ping gone, presence polled every 60 s with the screen off | Automated (the proxy, the default) / **Unproven** (battery on the handset) | `cd aura-adapter && python3 -m pytest tests -q -k models`; `gradle :app:testDebugUnitTest --tests "*WakeOnDeviceDefaultTest*"` |

## Closing the gaps — what only you can check

These need your hardware. None of them are covered above, and none of them
should be assumed to work until you have done them once.

### 1. The Kotlin actually builds

Needs an Android SDK with platform 35 and JDK 17+ (`android-app/README.md` has
the details). No wrapper jar is committed:

```bash
cd android-app
gradle wrapper              # once; writes gradlew and the wrapper jar
./gradlew :app:assembleDebug
```

Until this has run, treat the Android app as unbuilt source. If it builds,
install it and check the Tier-3 consent screen appears for a `CONFIRM` action
and that dismissing it runs nothing.

### 2. The HUD against a real jarvis-core

From the repository root:

```bash
TOKEN="$(cd aura-core && python3 -m aura --config ./config --create-token hud)"
( cd aura-core && python3 -m aura --config ./config --port 8080 ) &

cd aura-web && npm run build
AURA_BACKEND=core AURA_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8080 AURA_TOKEN="$TOKEN" node build
```

(Minting the token first is deliberate: it must be in the store before the
server loads it. This writes `aura-core/config/.storage/`, which is where
your real tokens and registries live — it is not currently in `.gitignore`, so
check `git status` before committing after doing this.)

Then open the HUD in a real browser and confirm: the orb renders and animates,
tap-to-talk captures the microphone, the transcript streams, and the reply plays
back as audio. Every one of those is unproven today.

### 3. The voice stack end to end, with a microphone

```bash
docker compose up -d          # whisper, piper, openWakeWord, the model gateway
./scripts/e2e-smoke.sh        # proves they are reachable and piper speaks
python3 scripts/pipeline-smoke.py   # the existing pipeline probe
```

Then say the wake word out loud and confirm detection, transcription accuracy
and reply latency. Wake-word *accuracy* — false accepts and false rejects in
your actual room, with your actual voice — is unproven and cannot be tested any
other way. `docs/how-to/wake-word-training.md` covers improving it.

### 4. Real devices

Point `mqtt:` at your broker and confirm Zigbee2MQTT/Tasmota/ESPHome devices
appear by discovery. Fill in `hue:` and `wled:` with real addresses and confirm
the entities go live and respond. All three are tested only against fakes.

### 5. The isolation claims

```bash
./scripts/egress-audit.sh              # sandbox has only lo; no LAN, no internet
DRY_RUN=1 ./scripts/apply-firewall.sh  # preview, then run for real
```

`docs/explanation/security.md` states these as properties of the deployment. They are true
only once these scripts have been run against the live stack and have passed.

### 6. Performance

Per-stage latency is measured on every live run (stt, first token, intent, tts, total; median and p95 in the live test report) and by the scorecard idle and under load (`m30-toolbelt.sh` (deleted in 23177d71)). GPU throughput and memory under load are not measured anywhere; there is no GPU on this box to measure.

---

## The harness

The per-milestone harness (`verify-all`, one script per milestone, logs in `.verify/`) was
deleted in 23177d71, with older gates and specs in acc05134, 0d02f0d8, bf8f6940, 964b8721,
33a05b66 and b164d0df. A deleted proof keeps its bare name and the commit that removed it.
A claim whose script, spec, test or job was retired has no proof in the tree and is not
listed: T-1522 took the 44 Retired rows and four retired sections out (git history keeps
them); write a test and the claim comes back as a row. What proves a row now is the command in its own
Proof cell; `python3 scripts/check-links.py` fails on one that names a path, test or
`make` target that does not exist. Since T-1441 that includes a test file cited by bare
name (a pytest file, a console spec or unit test, a Kotlin test class) that no tree has, and a suite
under a top folder that is gone
(`deploy/tests/test_pipeline.py::test_the_links_check_fails_a_register_row_citing_a_test_file_or_suite_that_is_gone`):
until then 73 citations of 25 deleted test files and four of two retired trees' suites
passed it, and 34 rows that rested on nothing else are marked Retired above, each with
the file and the commit. Not read for a bare name: which tree has the file, and whether
the test it names inside it exists.

There is deliberately **no skip state**: a check that cannot run fails. A suite
that reports green while proving nothing is worse than one that reports red,
because somebody believes it.

## Maintaining this document

Whenever the answer to "does this work?" changes, this file changes with it.
Two rules keep it worth reading:

### The audit's plan (M84–M98), as built on 27 Aug

Every row's gate has an offline half that is green on the branch and a live half that runs
against the rebuilt house (the fifteenth rebuild, queued behind the old `verify-all`); a row is
**Built** until its live half has passed there, and the tick in `MILESTONES.md` follows the
gate, never the other way round. The overnight log carries the times.

| Milestone | Level | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| M84 — Jarvis volunteers a briefing | Automated + Containerised (8/8 on 27 Aug 2026, the 10:21 house) | `m84-briefing.sh` (deleted in 23177d71); `test_features.py -k briefing` (20); scenario `briefing-on-demand` |
| M85 — Work survives a restart | **Withdrawn** (T-1499, 2026-11-17): the resume was never reachable (nothing registered a way to rebuild a worker), so it is removed and the claim with it | What a restart does now, and is tested to do: waiting work ends failed ("was waiting when Jarvis stopped, and nothing has it now"), running work ends interrupted and is not re-run, and a by-hand retry works only within one process. `cd aura-core && python3 -m pytest tests/test_taskengine.py tests/test_tasks.py -q` (17 + 27, the restart outcomes among them). The live scenario `task-survives-a-restart`, which expected a resumed job from the retired house agent, is removed with it; `m85-work-survives-a-restart.sh` was deleted in 23177d71. A scheduled job a restart cut short is the scheduler's own catch-up to settle |

### Media: the artifact store, the panels and the routing gate (2026-09-18)

The media brief's gates are benchmark tasks, not unit tests: what they assert is which tool the
model reached for, read off the tool-call trace. Five tasks under `bench/tasks/multimodal/`
carry them. **None has been run against the stack yet** — every task row below is Unproven
and says so; the only Automated rows are the harness's own. Run one with the environment
`bench/runner.py` `from_env` documents (`BENCH_EXEC="docker exec jarvis"` or the fixtures run
on the host and the run fails looking like a bench defect), the fixture server up first:

```bash
python3 bench/fixtures/server.py --port 8899 &
BENCH_EXEC="docker exec jarvis" python3 -m bench run --id media-fetch-007 --out bench/results/<name>
```

The runner's gateway session is a terminal client that reports no surface, so the routing
profile every one of these runs under is `surfaces.UNKNOWN` (downloads only) — the same
verdict `console` gets, and the premise media-handoff-010 needs. A client-reported surface
(`prompt.submit` carrying `params.surface`) is not exercised by any task here.

| Claim | Level | Proof / command |
|---|---|---|
| The runner can assert a *negative* on the trace: `tool_not_used`, one name or any-of, optionally only calls whose args carry a substring; a crashed run never passes it | Automated | `python3 -m pytest bench/tests -q` (375 on 2026-09-18; `test_tool_not_used_passes_only_when_no_matching_call`) |
| The five tasks load, carry the checker the brief's gate needs, gate on `plugin:aura-artifacts`, and every fixture they name is on disk (`bench/fixtures/assets/clip.mp4`, a two-second h264+aac clip; `bench/fixtures/documents/scan.djvu`; `bench/fixtures/documents/manual.txt`) | Automated | same command (`test_media_tasks_assert_the_brief_s_gates`, `test_referenced_fixture_assets_exist`); `CAPABILITIES.md` carries the five rows (`make capabilities`) |
| The store: a video fetched by URL is pulled gateway-side by `media_fetch`, published, and reaches the session's panel | **Unproven** | `… --id media-fetch-007`; passes on `tool_used: media_fetch` — a `yt-dlp` from `terminal` fails it, which is the brief's point (the shell has no network, ADR-0045). The tool is the M4 work; the row assumes its name |
